, then we compute

\frac{d}{dt}\varphi\circ\phi^{R}_{t}=D\varphi\circ R\circ\phi_{t}^{R}=\varphi_{*}R\circ\varphi\circ\phi_{t}^{R}=R\circ\varphi\circ\phi_{t}^{R},

for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. To prove
\varphi_{*}R=R
, just observe that

\displaystyle i_{\varphi_{*}R}d\alpha

\displaystyle=\varphi^{*}d\alpha(R\circ\varphi^{-1},D\varphi^{-1}\cdot)

\displaystyle=d\varphi^{*}\alpha(R\circ\varphi^{-1},D\varphi^{-1}\cdot)

\displaystyle=d\alpha(R\circ\varphi^{-1},D\varphi^{-1}\cdot)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{*}(i_{R}d\alpha)

\displaystyle=0,

and

\alpha(\varphi_{*}R)=\alpha(D\varphi\circ R\circ\varphi^{-1})=\varphi^{*}\alpha(R\circ\varphi^{-1})=\alpha(R\circ\varphi^{-1})=1.

Hence the statement follows from the uniqueness of integral curves.
∎

Proposition 3.2 (Kernel of the Hessian of the Twisted Rabinowitz Action Functional).

Let
(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})
be a regular energy surface of restricted contact type in an exact Hamiltonian system
(M,\lambda,H)
with
X_{H}|_{\Sigma}=R
. Suppose
\varphi\in\Diff(M)
is an exact symplectomorphism such that
H\in C^{\infty}_{\varphi}(M)
and
\varphi^{*}\lambda|_{\Sigma}=\lambda|_{\Sigma}
. Then

\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}=\mathscr{P}_{\varphi}(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})

and

\ker\Hess\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}\cong\ker\mathinner{\bigl(D(\phi^{R}_{-\tau}\circ\varphi)|_{\gamma(0)}-\id_{T_{\gamma(0)}\Sigma}\bigr)}

for all
(\gamma,\tau)\in\mathscr{P}_{\varphi}(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})
. Moreover, we have
R(\gamma(0))\in\ker\Hess\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}
and if
\mathscr{P}_{\varphi}(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})\subseteq\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
is an embedded submanifold, then
\Spec(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})
is discrete.

Remark 3.5.

If
(\gamma,\tau)\in\mathscr{P}_{\varphi}(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})
, we have the period-action equality

\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(\gamma,\tau)=\int_{0}^{1}\gamma^{*}\lambda=\int_{0}^{1}\lambda(\dot{\gamma})=\tau\int_{0}^{1}\lambda(R(\gamma))=\tau.

Proof.

The identity
\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}=\mathscr{P}_{\varphi}(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})
immediately follows from Corollary
3.2
together with
[
Lee12
, Corollary 5.30]
. The proof of the formula for the kernel of the Hessian of
\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
is inspired by
[
FK18
, p. 102]
. By Lemma
3.4
we have that

\ker\Hess\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}\cong\mathfrak{K}(\gamma,\tau),

where
(v_{0},\eta)\in T_{\gamma(0)}M\times\mathbb{R}
belongs to
\mathfrak{K}(\gamma,\tau)
if and only if

D(\phi^{X_{H}}_{-\tau}\circ\varphi)v_{0}=v_{0}+\eta X_{H}(\gamma(0))\hskip 22.99988pt\text{and}\hskip 22.99988ptdH(v_{0})=0.

Thus in our setting, the second condition implies
v_{0}\in T_{\gamma(0)}\Sigma
. Decompose

v_{0}=v^{\xi}_{0}+aR(\gamma(0))\hskip 22.99988ptv^{\xi}_{0}\in\xi_{\gamma(0)},a\in\mathbb{R},

where
\xi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\ker\lambda|_{\Sigma}
denotes the contact distribution. Then we compute

\displaystyle D(\phi^{R}_{-\tau}\circ\varphi)R(\gamma(0))

\displaystyle=D(\phi^{R}_{-\tau}\circ\varphi)\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}\phi^{R}_{t}(\gamma(0))\biggr)}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}(\phi^{R}_{-\tau}\circ\varphi\circ\phi^{R}_{t})(\gamma(0))

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}(\phi^{R}_{t}\circ\varphi\circ\phi^{R}_{-\tau})(\gamma(0))

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}\phi^{R}_{t}(\gamma(0))

\displaystyle=R(\gamma(0)),

using Lemma
3.5
. Hence

v_{0}+\eta R(\gamma(0))=D(\phi^{R}_{-\tau}\circ\varphi)v_{0}=D^{\xi}(\phi^{R}_{-\tau}\circ\varphi)v_{0}^{\xi}+aR(\gamma(0)),

where

D^{\xi}(\phi^{R}_{-\tau}\circ\varphi)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=D(\phi^{R}_{-\tau}\circ\varphi)|_{\xi}\colon\xi\to\xi,

implies

\eta=0\hskip 22.99988pt\text{and}\hskip 22.99988ptD^{\xi}(\phi^{R}_{-\tau}\circ\varphi)v_{0}^{\xi}=v_{0}^{\xi}

by considering the splitting
T\Sigma=\xi\oplus\langle R\rangle
. Consequently

\mathfrak{K}(\gamma,\tau)=\ker\mathinner{\bigl(D(\phi^{R}_{-\tau}\circ\varphi)|_{\gamma(0)}-\id_{T_{\gamma(0)}\Sigma}\bigr)}\times\mathinner{\{0\}}.

Finally, assume that
\mathscr{P}_{\varphi}(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})\subseteq\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
is an embedded submanifold via the obvious identification of
(\gamma,\tau)\in\mathscr{P}_{\varphi}(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})
with
(\gamma(0),\tau)\in\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
. Fix a path
(\gamma_{s},\tau_{s})
in
\mathscr{P}_{\varphi}(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})=\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
from
(\gamma_{0},\tau_{0})
to
(\gamma_{1},\tau_{1})
. Using Remark
3.5
we compute

\partial_{s}\tau_{s}=\partial_{s}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(\gamma_{s},\tau_{s})=d\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma_{s},\tau_{s})}(\partial_{s}\gamma_{s},\partial_{s}\tau_{s})=0,

implying that
\tau_{s}
is constant, and in particular
\tau_{0}=\tau_{1}
. Consequently,
\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
is constant on each path-connected component of
\mathscr{P}_{\varphi}(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})
. As
\mathscr{P}_{\varphi}(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})
is a submanifold of
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
, there are only countably many connected components by definition, implying that
\Spec(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})
is discrete.
∎

3.2
Compactness of the Moduli Space of Twisted Negative Gradient Flow Lines

Definition 3.10 (Twisted Defining Hamiltonian Function).

Let
(W,\lambda)
be a Liouville domain with boundary
\Sigma
and
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
. A
twisted defining Hamiltonian function for
\Sigma
is a Hamiltonian function
H\in C^{\infty}(M)
on the completion
(M,\lambda)
of
(W,\lambda)
, satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

H^{-1}(0)=\Sigma
and
\Sigma\cap\Crit H=\varnothing
.

(ii)

H\in C^{\infty}_{\varphi}(M)
.

(iii)

dH
is compactly supported.

(iv)

X_{H}|_{\Sigma}=R
is the Reeb vector field of the contact form
\lambda|_{\Sigma}
.

Denote by
\mathscr{F}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)
the set of twisted defining Hamiltonian functions for
\Sigma
.

Remark 3.6.

A necessary condition for
\mathscr{F}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)\neq\varnothing
is that
\varphi^{*}R=R
. This is not true in general if
\varphi
does not induce a strict contactomorphism on
\Sigma
.

Definition 3.11 (Adapted Almost Complex Structure).

Let
(W,\lambda)
be a Liouville domain with boundary
\Sigma
. An
adapted almost complex structure on
(W,\lambda)
is a
d\lambda
-compatible almost complex structure
J
on
(W,\lambda)
such that
J
restricts to define a compatible almost complex structure on the contact distribution
\ker\lambda|_{\Sigma}
and
JR=\partial_{r}
holds near the boundary.

Definition 3.12 (Rabinowitz–Floer Data).

Let
(M,\lambda)
be the completion of a Liouville domain
(W,\lambda)
with boundary
\Sigma
and
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
.
Rabinowitz–Floer data for
\varphi
is defined to be a pair
(H,J)
consisting of a twisted defining Hamiltonian function
H\in\mathscr{F}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)
for
\Sigma
and an adapted almost complex structure
J
on
(W,\lambda)
such that
\varphi^{*}J=J
.

Lemma 3.6.

Let
(W,\lambda)
be a Liouville domain and
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
. Then there exists Rabinowitz–Floer data for
\varphi
.

Proof.

The construction of the twisted defining Hamiltonian
H
for
\Sigma
is inspired by the proof of
[
CFP10
, Proposition 4.1]
. Fix
\delta>0
such that the exact symplectic embedding

\psi\colon\mathinner{\bigl(\mathinner{(-\delta,0]}\times\Sigma,e^{r}\lambda|_{\Sigma}\bigr)}\hookrightarrow(W,\lambda)

defined by

\psi(r,x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\phi^{X}_{r}(x)

satisfies

U_{\delta}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\psi(\mathinner{(-\delta,0]}\times\Sigma)\cap\supp f_{\varphi}=\varnothing.

(26)

Indeed, that
\psi
is an exact symplectic embedding follows from the computation

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dr}\psi^{*}_{r}\lambda

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dr}\mathinner{\bigl(\phi_{r}^{X}\bigr)}^{*}\lambda

\displaystyle=(\phi^{X}_{r})^{*}L_{X}\lambda

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\bigl(\phi_{r}^{X}\bigr)}^{*}(di_{X}\lambda+i_{X}d\lambda)

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\bigl(\phi_{r}^{X}\bigr)}^{*}(di_{X}i_{X}d\lambda+\lambda)

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\bigl(\phi_{r}^{X}\bigr)}^{*}\lambda

\displaystyle=\psi^{*}_{r}\lambda

implying

\psi^{*}_{r}\lambda=e^{r}\lambda|_{\Sigma}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall r\in\mathinner{(-\delta,0]},

by
\psi_{0}=\iota_{\Sigma}
, where
\iota_{\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\hookrightarrow W
denotes the inclusion. Note that
\psi^{*}_{r}X=\partial_{r}
. We claim

\varphi(\psi(r,x))=\psi(r,\varphi(x))\hskip 22.99988pt\forall(r,x)\in\mathinner{(-\delta,0]}\times\Sigma,

(27)

that is,
\varphi
and
\psi
commute. Note that (
27
) is well-defined since
\varphi(\Sigma)=\Sigma
by assumption. Indeed, (
27
) follows from the uniqueness of integral curves and the computation

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dr}\varphi(\psi(r,x))

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dr}\varphi(\phi_{r}^{X}(x))

\displaystyle=D\varphi(X(\phi_{r}^{X}(x)))

\displaystyle=(D\varphi\circ X|_{U_{\delta}}\circ\varphi^{-1}\circ\varphi)(\phi_{r}^{X}(x))

\displaystyle=(\varphi_{*}X|_{\varphi(U_{\delta})}\circ\varphi)(\phi_{r}^{X}(x))

\displaystyle=(X|_{\varphi(U_{\delta})}\circ\varphi)(\phi_{r}^{X}(x))

\displaystyle=X(\varphi(\psi(r,x)))

where we used the
\varphi
-invariance of the Liouville vector field on
U_{\delta}
, that is

\varphi_{*}X|_{\varphi(U_{\delta})}=X|_{\varphi(U_{\delta})},

which in turn follows from

\displaystyle i_{\varphi_{*}X}d\lambda

\displaystyle=d\lambda(\varphi_{*}X,\cdot)

\displaystyle=d\lambda(D\varphi\circ X\circ\varphi^{-1},\cdot)

\displaystyle=d\lambda\mathinner{\bigl(D\varphi\circ X\circ\varphi^{-1},D\varphi\circ D\varphi^{-1}\cdot\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\varphi^{*}d\lambda(X\circ\varphi^{-1},D\varphi^{-1}\cdot)

\displaystyle=d\varphi^{*}\lambda(X\circ\varphi^{-1},D\varphi^{-1}\cdot)

\displaystyle=d\lambda(X\circ\varphi^{-1},D\varphi^{-1}\cdot)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{*}(i_{X}d\lambda)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{*}\lambda

\displaystyle=\lambda-d(f_{\varphi}\circ\varphi^{-1})

and assumption (
26
).

Next we construct the defining Hamiltonian
H\in C^{\infty}(M)
. Let
h\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be a sufficiently small mollification of the piecewise linear function

h(r)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\begin{cases}r&r\in\mathinner{\bigl[-\frac{\delta}{2},\frac{\delta}{2}\bigr]},\\
\frac{\delta}{2}&r\in\mathinner{\bigl[\frac{\delta}{2},+\infty\bigr)},\\
-\frac{\delta}{2}&r\in\mathinner{\bigl(-\infty,-\frac{\delta}{2}\bigr]},\end{cases}

as in Figure
8
.

Figure 8
:
Mollification of the piecewise linear function
h
.

Define
H\in C^{\infty}(M)
by

H(p)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\begin{cases}h(r)&p=\psi(r,x)\in U_{\delta},\\
h(r)&p=(r,x)\in\mathinner{[0,+\infty)}\times\Sigma,\\
-\frac{\delta}{2}&p\in W\setminus U_{\delta}.\end{cases}

(28)

Then
H
is a defining Hamiltonian for
\Sigma
and
dH
is compactly supported by construction. Moreover,
H
is
\varphi
-invariant by (
27
). Finally,
X_{H}|_{\Sigma}=R
follows from the observation
X_{H}=h^{\prime}(r)e^{-r}R
. Indeed, on
U_{\delta}
we compute

\displaystyle i_{h^{\prime}(t)e^{-r}R}\psi^{*}d\lambda

\displaystyle=i_{h^{\prime}(r)e^{-r}R}d(e^{r}\lambda|_{\Sigma})

\displaystyle=i_{h^{\prime}(r)e^{-r}R}(e^{r}dr\wedge\lambda|_{\Sigma}+e^{r}d\lambda|_{\Sigma})

\displaystyle=-h^{\prime}(r)dr

\displaystyle=-dH.

Next we construct the adapted almost complex structure
J
. Fix a
\varphi
-invariant compatible almost complex structure
J^{\Sigma}
on the contact distribution
\ker\lambda|_{\Sigma}
. Extend this family to
(\mathinner{(-\delta,+\infty)}\times\Sigma,d(e^{r}\lambda|_{\Sigma}))
by setting

J^{\Sigma}|_{(a,x)}(b,v)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\bigl(\lambda_{x}(v),J^{\Sigma}|_{x}(\pi(v))-bR(x)\bigr)},

(29)

where
\pi\colon\ker\lambda|_{\Sigma}\oplus\langle R\rangle\to\ker\lambda|_{\Sigma}
denotes the projection. Choose a
\varphi
-invariant
d\lambda
-compatible almost complex structure
J^{W\setminus\Sigma}
on
W\setminus\Sigma
and let
\mathinner{\bigl\{\beta^{\Sigma},\beta^{W\setminus\Sigma}\bigr\}}
be a partition of unity subordinate to the open cover
\mathinner{\{U_{\delta},W\setminus\Sigma\}}
of
W
. The compatible almost complex structure
J
associated with the Riemannian metric

m_{J}(\cdot,\cdot)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\beta^{\Sigma}d\lambda(J^{\Sigma}\cdot,\cdot)+\beta^{W\setminus\Sigma}d\lambda(J^{W\setminus\Sigma}\cdot,\cdot)

on
W
is adapted.
∎

Definition 3.13 (
L^{2}
-Metric).

Let
(H,J)
be Rabinowitz–Floer data for a Liouville automorphism
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
. Define an
L^{2}
-metric
on
\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}M\times\mathbb{R}

\langle(X,\eta),(Y,\sigma)\rangle_{J}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\int_{0}^{1}d\lambda(JX(t),Y(t))dt+\eta\sigma

(30)

for all
(X,\eta),(Y,\sigma)\in T_{\gamma}\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}M\times\mathbb{R}
and
\gamma\in\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}M
.

With respect to the
L^{2}
-metric (
41
), the gradient of the twisted Rabinowitz action functional
\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}M\times\mathbb{R})
is given by

\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}(t)=\begin{pmatrix}J(\dot{\gamma}(t)-\tau X_{H}(\gamma(t)))\\
\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{1}H\circ\gamma\end{pmatrix}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall t\in\mathbb{R}.

Lemma 3.7 (Fundamental Lemma).

Let
(H,J)
be Rabinowitz–Floer data for a Liouville automorphism
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
of a Liouville domain
(W,\lambda)
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(\lambda,H,J,f_{\varphi})>0
such that

\mathinner{\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}\rVert}_{J}<\frac{1}{C}\hskip 11.49994pt\Rightarrow\hskip 11.49994pt\mathinner{\lvert\tau\rvert}\leq C(\mathinner{\lvert\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(\gamma,\tau)\rvert}+1)

for all
(\gamma,\tau)\in\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}M\times\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

We proceed in three steps.

Step 1: There exist constants
\delta>0
and
0<C_{\delta}<+\infty
such that if
(\gamma,\tau)\in\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}M
with
\gamma(I)\subseteq H^{-1}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathinner{(-\delta,\delta)}\bigr)}=\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}U_{\delta}
, then

\mathinner{\lvert\tau\rvert}\leq 2\mathinner{\lvert\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(\gamma,\tau)\rvert}+C_{\delta}\mathinner{\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}\rVert}_{J}+2\mathinner{\lVert f_{\varphi}\rVert}_{\infty}.

Choose
\delta>0
such that
U_{\delta}\subseteq\supp X_{H}
and

\lambda_{x}(X_{H}(x))\geq\frac{1}{2}+\delta\hskip 22.99988pt\forall x\in U_{\delta}.

This is possible as
X_{H}|_{\Sigma}=R
. Moreover, set

C_{\delta}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=2\mathinner{\lVert\lambda|_{U_{\delta}}\rVert}_{\infty}.

Then
C_{\delta}<+\infty
as
dH
is compactly supported. We estimate

\displaystyle\mathinner{\lvert\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(\gamma,\tau)\rvert}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\int_{0}^{1}\gamma^{*}\lambda-\tau\int_{0}^{1}H(\gamma)-f_{\varphi}(\gamma(0))\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\tau\int_{0}^{1}\lambda(X_{H}(\gamma))+\int_{0}^{1}\lambda(\dot{\gamma}-\tau X_{H}(\gamma))-\tau\int_{0}^{1}H(\gamma)-f_{\varphi}(\gamma(0))\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle\geq\mathinner{\lvert\tau\rvert}\mathinner{\biggl(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\biggr)}-\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\int_{0}^{1}\lambda(\dot{\gamma}-\tau X_{H}(\gamma))\biggr\rvert}-\mathinner{\lvert\tau\rvert}\delta-\mathinner{\lVert f_{\varphi}\rVert}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathinner{\lvert\tau\rvert}}{2}-\frac{C_{\delta}}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\mathinner{\lVert\dot{\gamma}(t)-\tau X_{H}(\gamma(t))\rVert}_{J}dt-\mathinner{\lVert f_{\varphi}\rVert}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathinner{\lvert\tau\rvert}}{2}-\frac{C_{\delta}}{2}\sqrt{\int_{0}^{1}\mathinner{\lVert\dot{\gamma}(t)-\tau X_{H}(\gamma(t))\rVert}^{2}_{J}dt}-\mathinner{\lVert f_{\varphi}\rVert}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathinner{\lvert\tau\rvert}}{2}-\frac{C_{\delta}}{2}\mathinner{\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}\rVert}_{J}-\mathinner{\lVert f_{\varphi}\rVert}_{\infty}

by Jensen’s inequality.

Step 2: For all
\delta>0
, there exists
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\delta)>0
with the property that if there exists
t_{0}\in I
with
\mathinner{\lvert H(\gamma(t_{0}))\rvert}\geq\delta
for
(\gamma,\tau)\in\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}M
, then
\mathinner{\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}\rVert}_{J}\geq\varepsilon
.

Assume first that
\gamma(t)\in M\setminus U_{\frac{\delta}{2}}
for all
t\in I
. In this case we estimate

\mathinner{\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}\rVert}_{J}\geq\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\int_{0}^{1}H(\gamma)\biggr\rvert}\geq\frac{\delta}{2}.

Otherwise, we may assume without loss of generality that there exists
t_{1}\in I
such that
\mathinner{\lvert H(\gamma(t_{1}))\rvert}\leq\frac{\delta}{2}
,
t_{0}<t_{1}
and
\frac{\delta}{2}\leq\mathinner{\lvert H(\gamma(t))\rvert}\leq\delta
for all
t\in\mathinner{[t_{0},t_{1}]}
. Set

\kappa\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\max_{x\in\overline{U}_{\delta}}\mathinner{\lVert\grad_{J}H\rVert}_{J}>0,

as
dH\neq 0
in a neighbourhood of
\Sigma
. We estimate

\displaystyle\mathinner{\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}\rVert}_{J}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{\int_{0}^{1}\mathinner{\lVert\dot{\gamma}(t)-\tau X_{H}(\gamma(t))\rVert}_{J}^{2}dt}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{1}\mathinner{\lVert\dot{\gamma}(t)-\tau X_{H}(\gamma(t))\rVert}_{J}dt

\displaystyle\geq\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\mathinner{\lVert\dot{\gamma}(t)-\tau X_{H}(\gamma(t))\rVert}_{J}dt

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\kappa}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\mathinner{\lVert\grad_{J}H(\gamma(t))\rVert}_{J}\mathinner{\lVert\dot{\gamma}(t)-\tau X_{H}(\gamma(t))\rVert}_{J}dt

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\kappa}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\mathinner{\lvert m_{J}(\grad_{J}H(\gamma(t)),\dot{\gamma}(t)-\tau X_{H}(\gamma(t)))\rvert}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\mathinner{\lvert dH(\dot{\gamma}(t)-\tau X_{H}(\gamma(t)))\rvert}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\mathinner{\lvert dH(\dot{\gamma}(t))\rvert}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\frac{d}{dt}(H\circ\gamma)\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\kappa}\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\frac{d}{dt}(H\circ\gamma)\biggr\rvert}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa}\mathinner{\lvert H(\gamma(t_{1}))-H(\gamma(t_{0}))\rvert}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\kappa}\mathinner{\bigl(\mathinner{\lvert H(\gamma(t_{0}))\rvert}-\mathinner{\lvert H(\gamma(t_{1}))\rvert}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\delta}{2\kappa}

by Cauchy–Schwarz. Hence
\mathinner{\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}\rVert}_{J}\geq\varepsilon
for

\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\delta)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\delta}{2\max\mathinner{\{1,\kappa\}}}.

Step 3: We prove the Fundamental Lemma.
Choose
\delta>0
and
0<C_{\delta}<+\infty
as in Step 1 and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\delta)>0
as in Step 2. Set

C_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\max\mathinner{\bigl\{2,C_{\delta}\varepsilon+2\mathinner{\lVert f_{\varphi}\rVert}_{\infty}\bigr\}}.

Assume that
\mathinner{\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}\rVert}_{J}<\varepsilon
for
(\gamma,\tau)\in\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}M
. Then
\gamma(I)\subseteq U_{\delta}
, as otherwise there exists
t_{0}\in I
with
\mathinner{\lvert H(\gamma(t_{0}))\rvert}\geq\delta
implying
\mathinner{\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(\gamma,\tau)}\rVert}_{J}\geq\varepsilon
by Step 2. Thus with Step 1 we estimate

\mathinner{\lvert\tau\rvert}\leq C_{0}(\mathinner{\lvert\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(\gamma,\tau)\rvert}+1).

(31)

Finally, set

C\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\max\mathinner{\biggl\{C_{0},\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\biggr\}}.

This proves the Fundamental Lemma.
∎

Definition 3.14 (Twisted Negative Gradient Flow Line).

Let
(H,J)
be Rabinowitz–Floer data for a Liouville automorphism
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
. A
twisted negative gradient flow line
is a tuple
(u,\tau)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}M\times\mathbb{R})
such that

\partial_{s}(u,\tau)=-\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(u(s),\tau(s))}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall s\in\mathbb{R}.

Definition 3.15 (Energy).

Let
(H,J)
be Rabinowitz–Floer data for a Liouville automorphism
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
. The
energy of a twisted negative gradient flow line
(u,\tau)
is defined by

E_{J}(u,\tau)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\mathinner{\lVert\partial_{s}(u,\tau)\rVert}^{2}_{J}ds=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(u(s),\tau(s))}\bigr\rVert}^{2}_{J}ds.

Theorem 3.1 (Compactness).

Let
(H,J)
be Rabinowitz–Floer data for a Liouville automorphism
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
. Suppose
(u_{k},\tau_{k})
is a sequence of negative gradient flow lines of the twisted Rabinowitz action functional
\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
such that there exist constants
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
with

a\leq\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}\mathinner{\bigl(u_{k}(s),\tau_{k}(s)\bigr)}\leq b\hskip 22.99988pt\forall k\in\mathbb{N},s\in\mathbb{R}.

For every reparametrisation sequence
(s_{k})\subseteq\mathbb{R}
there exists a subsequence
(s_{k_{l}})
and a negative gradient flow line
(u_{\infty},\tau_{\infty})
of
\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
such that

\mathinner{\bigl(u_{k_{l}}(\cdot+s_{k_{l}}),\tau_{k_{l}}(\cdot+s_{k_{l}})\bigr)}\xrightarrow{C^{\infty}_{\loc}}(u_{\infty},\tau_{\infty})\hskip 22.99988pt\text{as }l\to\infty.

Proof.

In order to show
C^{\infty}_{\loc}
-convergence, we need to establish

•

a uniform
L^{\infty}
-bound on
u_{k}
,

•

a uniform
L^{\infty}
-bound on
\tau_{k}
,

•

a uniform
L^{\infty}
-bound on the derivatives of
u_{k}
.

Indeed, through elliptic bootstrapping
[
MS12
, Theorem B.4.1]
the negative gradient flow equation, we will obtain
C^{\infty}_{\loc}
-convergence by
[
MS12
, Theorem B.4.2]
. To obtain a uniform
L^{\infty}
-bound on the sequence of twisted negative gradient flow lines
u_{k}
, observe that by definition of Rabinowitz–Floer data for
\varphi
, there exists
r\in\mathinner{(0,+\infty)}
such that

\supp X_{H}\cap\mathinner{[r,+\infty)}\times\Sigma=\varnothing

and
J
is adapted to the boundary of
W\cup_{\Sigma}\mathinner{[0,r]}\times\Sigma
. Consequently, the Maximum Principle
[
MS12
, Corollary 9.2.11]
implies that every
u_{k}
remains inside the compact set
W\cup_{\Sigma}\mathinner{[0,r]}\times\Sigma
as the asymptotics belong to
W\cup_{\Sigma}\mathinner{[0,r)}\times\Sigma
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Indeed, this follows from

\displaystyle E_{J}(u_{k},\tau_{k})

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\mathinner{\lVert\partial_{s}(u_{k},\tau_{k})\rVert}^{2}_{J}ds

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\langle\partial_{s}(u_{k},\tau_{k}),\partial_{s}(u_{k},\tau_{k})\rangle_{J}ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\langle\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(u_{k}(s),\tau_{k}(s))},\partial_{s}(u_{k},\tau_{k})\rangle_{J}ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}d\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(\partial_{s}(u_{k},\tau_{k}))ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\partial_{s}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(u_{k},\tau_{k})ds

\displaystyle=\lim_{s\to-\infty}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(u_{k}(s),\tau_{k}(s))-\lim_{s\to+\infty}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(u_{k}(s),\tau_{k}(s))

\displaystyle\leq b-a,

as this implies

\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\mathinner{\lVert\partial_{s}(u_{k},\tau_{k})\rVert}_{J}=\lim_{s\to\pm\infty}\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(u_{k}(s),\tau_{k}(s))}\bigr\rVert}_{J}=0

by the negative gradient flow equation.

The uniform
L^{\infty}
-bound on the Lagrange multipliers
\tau_{k}
follows from the Fundamental Lemma
3.7
. Fix a twisted negative gradient flow line
(u,\tau)
and let
C>0
as in the Fundamental Lemma
3.7
. For every
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
we can define
\zeta(\sigma)\geq 0
by

\zeta(\sigma)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\inf\mathinner{\biggl\{\zeta\geq 0\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(u(\sigma+\zeta),\tau(\sigma+\zeta))}\bigr\rVert}_{J}<\frac{1}{C}\biggr\}}.

We estimate

b-a\geq E_{J}(u,\tau)\geq\int_{\sigma}^{\sigma+\zeta(\sigma)}\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(u_{s},\tau(s))}\bigr\rVert}^{2}_{J}ds\geq\frac{\zeta(\sigma)}{C^{2}}.

By the Fundamental Lemma
3.7
we have that

\mathinner{\lvert\tau(\sigma+\zeta(\sigma))\rvert}<C(\max\{\mathinner{\lvert a\rvert},\mathinner{\lvert b\rvert}\}+1)\hskip 22.99988pt\forall\sigma\in\mathbb{R},

and thus using the negative gradient flow equation again we estimate

\displaystyle\mathinner{\lvert\tau(\sigma)\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\lvert\tau(\sigma+\zeta(\sigma))\rvert}+\int_{\sigma}^{\sigma+\zeta(\sigma)}\mathinner{\lvert\partial_{s}\tau(s)\rvert}ds

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\lvert\tau(\sigma+\zeta(\sigma))\rvert}+\int_{\sigma}^{\sigma+\zeta(\sigma)}\mathinner{\!\biggl\lvert\int_{0}^{1}H(u(s,t))dt\biggr\rvert}ds

\displaystyle\leq C(\max\{\mathinner{\lvert a\rvert},\mathinner{\lvert b\rvert}\}+1)+\zeta(\sigma)\mathinner{\lVert H\rVert}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C(\max\{\mathinner{\lvert a\rvert},\mathinner{\lvert b\rvert}\}+1)+C^{2}(b-a)\mathinner{\lVert H\rVert}_{\infty}.

for all
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
. Hence

\mathinner{\lVert\tau\rVert}_{\infty}\leq C(\max\{\mathinner{\lvert a\rvert},\mathinner{\lvert b\rvert}\}+1)+C^{2}(b-a)\mathinner{\lVert H\rVert}_{\infty}

is independent of the twisted negative gradient flow line
(u,\tau)
.

The uniform
L^{\infty}
-bound on the derivatives of
u_{k}
follows from Corollary
D.1
as an exact symplectic manifold is symplectically aspherical.
∎

3.3
Definition of Twisted Rabinowitz–Floer Homology

In this section we make implicit use of the requirement that a Liouville automorphism has finite order. This is crucial because then the arguments proceed as in the case of loops by Remark
3.2
.

Definition 3.16 (Transverse Conley–Zehnder Index).

Let
(W^{2n},\lambda)
be a Liouville domain with boundary
\Sigma
. Let
(\gamma_{0},\tau_{0}),(\gamma_{1},\tau_{1})\in\mathscr{P}_{\varphi}(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})
for some Liouville automorphism
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
such that there exists a path
\gamma_{\sigma}
in
\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}\Sigma
from
\gamma_{0}
to
\gamma_{1}
. Define the
transverse Conley–Zehnder index
by

\mu((\gamma_{0},\tau_{0}),(\gamma_{1},\tau_{1}))\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mu_{\CZ}(\Psi^{1})-\mu_{\CZ}(\Psi^{0})\in\mathbb{Z},

with

\displaystyle\Psi^{0}\colon I\to\Sp(n-1),\hskip 22.99988pt

\displaystyle\Psi^{0}_{t}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Phi_{t,0}^{-1}\circ D^{\xi}\phi^{R}_{\tau_{0}t}\circ\Phi_{0,0},

\displaystyle\Psi^{1}\colon I\to\Sp(n-1),\hskip 22.99988pt

\displaystyle\Psi^{1}_{t}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Phi_{t,1}^{-1}\circ D^{\xi}\phi^{R}_{\tau_{1}t}\circ\Phi_{0,1},

where
\Phi_{t,\sigma}\colon\mathbb{R}^{2n-2}\to\xi_{\gamma_{\sigma}(t)}
is a symplectic trivialisation of
F^{*}\xi
,
\xi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\ker\lambda|_{\Sigma}
with
F\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times I,M)
being defined by
F(t,\sigma)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\gamma_{\sigma}(t)
, satisfying

\Phi_{t+1,\sigma}=D\varphi\circ\Phi_{t,\sigma}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall(t,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}\times I.

(32)

Lemma 3.8.

In the setup of Definition
3.16
, the transverse Conley–Zehnder index is well-defined, that is, independent of the choice of symplectic trivialisation.

Proof.

First we need to show that one can always construct a symplectic trivialisation

\Phi_{t,\sigma}\colon\mathbb{R}^{2n-2}\to\xi_{F(t,\sigma)}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall(t,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}\times I,

of
F^{*}\xi
satisfying the twist condition (
32
). By
[
MS17
, Theorem 2.1.3]
, there exists a linear symplectomorphism
\Phi_{0,0}\colon\mathbb{R}^{2n-2}\to\xi_{F(0,0)}
. By
[
MS17
, Lemma 2.6.6]
, we get a symplectic trivialisation
\Phi_{t,0}\colon\mathbb{R}^{2n-2}\to\xi_{F(t,0)}
for all
t\in I
with
\Phi_{1,0}=D\varphi\circ\Phi_{0,0}
. Extend this trivialisation to
\mathbb{R}
by setting

\Phi_{t+k,0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=D\varphi^{k}\circ\Phi_{t,0}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}.

Next, trivialise along each ray
\sigma\mapsto F(t,\sigma)
for fixed
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Hence we get a symplectic trivialisation
\Phi_{t,\sigma}\colon\mathbb{R}^{2n-2}\to\xi_{F(t,\sigma)}
of
F^{*}\xi
satisfying (
32
).

Now we show that the transverse Conley–Zehnder index
\mu
is independent of the choice of trivialisation.
Suppose that
\widetilde{\Phi}_{t,\sigma}\colon\mathbb{R}^{2n-2}\to\xi_{F(t,\sigma)}
is another symplectic trivialisation of
F^{*}\xi
satisfying

\widetilde{\Phi}_{t+1,\sigma}=D\varphi\circ\widetilde{\Phi}_{t,\sigma}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall(t,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}\times I.

Then we have

\widetilde{\Psi}_{t,\sigma}=\widetilde{\Phi}_{t,\sigma}^{-1}\circ\Phi_{t,\sigma}\circ\Psi_{t,\sigma}\circ\Phi_{0,\sigma}^{-1}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}_{0,\sigma}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall(t,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}\times\partial I,

where

\Psi_{t,\sigma}=\Phi_{t,\sigma}^{-1}\circ D^{\xi}\theta^{\tau_{\sigma}R}_{t}\circ\Phi_{0,\sigma}.

Define

\eta\colon\mathbb{T}\times I\to\Sp(n-1),\hskip 22.99988pt\eta(t,\sigma)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\Phi_{0,\sigma}^{-1}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}_{0,\sigma}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}^{-1}_{t,\sigma}\circ\Phi_{t,\sigma}.

Indeed, we compute

\displaystyle\eta(t+1,\sigma)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{0,\sigma}^{-1}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}_{0,\sigma}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}^{-1}_{t+1,\sigma}\circ\Phi_{t+1,\sigma}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{0,\sigma}^{-1}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}_{0,\sigma}\circ\widetilde{\Phi}^{-1}_{t,\sigma}\circ D\varphi^{-1}\circ D\varphi\circ\Phi_{t,\sigma}

\displaystyle=\eta(t,\sigma),

for all
(t,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}\times I
. Using the naturality as well as the loop property
[
Sal99
, p. 20]
of the Conley–Zehnder index we compute

\mu_{\CZ}(\widetilde{\Psi}^{s})=\mu_{\CZ}(\eta_{s}\cdot\Psi^{s})=\mu_{\CZ}(\Psi^{s})+2\mu_{\M}(\eta_{s})\hskip 22.99988pt\forall s\in\partial I,

where
\mu_{\M}
denotes the Maslov index. In particular,

\displaystyle\mu_{\CZ}(\widetilde{\Psi}^{1})-\mu_{\CZ}(\widetilde{\Psi}^{0})

\displaystyle=\mu_{\CZ}(\Psi^{1})-\mu_{\CZ}(\Psi^{0})+2\mathinner{\bigl(\mu_{\M}(\eta_{1})-\mu_{\M}(\eta_{0})\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\mu_{\CZ}(\Psi^{1})-\mu_{\CZ}(\Psi^{0})

by the invariance property of the Maslov index
[
FK18
, p. 195]
.
∎

Remark 3.7.

Denote by

\Sigma_{\varphi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}}{(\varphi(x),t+1){\sim}(x,t)}

the mapping torus of
\varphi
giving rise to the fibration

\pi_{\varphi}\colon\Sigma_{\varphi}\to\mathbb{T},\hskip 22.99988pt\pi_{\varphi}([x,t])\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=[t].

The vertical bundle
\ker D^{\xi}\pi_{\varphi}\to\Sigma_{\varphi}
is a symplectic vector bundle. If
\widetilde{F}
is another homotopy in
\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}\Sigma
from
\gamma_{0}
to
\gamma_{1}
, the concatenation with the reversed path
F^{-}
can be identified with the map

\widetilde{F}\#F^{-}\colon\mathbb{T}^{2}\to\Sigma_{\varphi},\hskip 22.99988pt(t,\sigma)\mapsto[(\widetilde{F}\#F^{-}(t,\sigma),t)].

Hence using the concatenation property of the Conley–Zehnder index
[
FK18
, p. 195]
as well as the functoriality of the Chern number
[
MS17
, p. 85]
, for
s\in\partial I
we compute

\displaystyle\mu_{\CZ}(\widetilde{\Psi}^{s})-\mu_{\CZ}(\Psi^{s})

\displaystyle=\mu_{\CZ}\mathinner{\bigl(\widetilde{\Psi}^{s}\#(\Psi^{s})^{-}\bigr)}

\displaystyle=2\mu_{\M}\mathinner{\bigl(\widetilde{\Psi}^{s}\#(\Psi^{s})^{-}\bigr)}

\displaystyle=2c_{1}\mathinner{\bigl((\widetilde{F}\#F^{-})^{*}\ker D^{\xi}\pi_{\varphi}\bigr)}

\displaystyle=2c_{1}(\ker D^{\xi}\pi_{\varphi}).

Thus if the transverse Conley–Zehnder index is viewed in
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
or
c_{1}(\ker D^{\xi}\pi_{\varphi})=0
, then it additionally does not depend on the choice of path in
\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}\Sigma
.

Let
(H,J)
be Rabinowitz–Floer data for
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
. Set

\Sigma\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\partial W\hskip 22.99988pt\text{and}\hskip 22.99988ptM\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=W\cup_{\Sigma}\mathinner{[0,+\infty)}\times\Sigma.

Fix
(\eta,\tau_{\eta})\in\mathscr{P}_{\varphi}(\Sigma,\lambda|_{\Sigma})
and denote by
[\eta]
the corresponding class in
\pi_{0}\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}\Sigma
. Assume that the twisted Rabinowitz action functional
\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
is Morse–Bott, that is,
\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}\subseteq\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
is a properly embedded submanifold by Proposition
3.2
, and fix a Morse function
h\in C^{\infty}(\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi})
. Define the twisted Rabinowitz–Floer chain group
\RFC^{\varphi}(\Sigma,M)
to be the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-vector space consisting of all formal linear combinations

\zeta=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\gamma,\tau)\in\Crit(h)\\
{[\gamma]=[\eta]}\end{subarray}}\zeta_{(\gamma,\tau)}(\gamma,\tau)

satisfying the Novikov finiteness condition

\#\mathinner{\{(\gamma,\tau)\in\Crit(h)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\zeta_{(\gamma,\tau)}\neq 0,\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(\gamma,\tau)\geq\kappa\}}<\infty\hskip 22.99988pt\forall\kappa\in\mathbb{R}.

Define a boundary operator

\partial\colon\RFC^{\varphi}(\Sigma,M)\to\RFC^{\varphi}(\Sigma,M)

by

\partial(\gamma^{-},\tau^{-})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\gamma^{+},\tau^{+})\in\Crit(h)\\
{[\gamma^{+}]=[\gamma^{-}]}\end{subarray}}n_{\varphi}(\gamma^{\pm},\tau^{\pm})(\gamma^{+},\tau^{+}),

where

n_{\varphi}(\gamma^{\pm},\tau^{\pm})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\#_{2}\mathscr{M}^{0}_{\varphi}(\gamma^{\pm},\tau^{\pm})\in\mathbb{Z}_{2},

with
\mathscr{M}^{0}_{\varphi}(\gamma^{\pm},\tau^{\pm})
denoting the zero-dimensional component of the moduli space of all unparametrised twisted negative gradient flow lines with cascades from
(\gamma^{-},\tau^{-})
to
(\gamma^{+},\tau^{+})
. This is well-defined by Theorem
3.1
. Define the
twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology of
\Sigma
and
\varphi
by

\RFH^{\varphi}(\Sigma,M)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\ker\partial}{\im\partial}.

Proposition 3.3.

Let
(W,\lambda)
be a Liouville domain with simply connected boundary
\Sigma
and
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
. If there do not exist any nonconstant twisted periodic Reeb orbits on
\Sigma
, then

\RFH^{\varphi}_{\ast}(\Sigma,M)\cong\operatorname{H}_{\ast}(\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma});\mathbb{Z}_{2}).

Proof.

If there do not exist any nonconstant twisted periodic Reeb orbits,

\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}=\mathinner{\{(c_{x},0)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma})\}}\cong\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma})

for any
H\in\mathscr{F}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)
. Since
\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma})
is a properly embedded submanifold of
\Sigma
by
[
Lee18
, Problem 8-32]
or
[
MS17
, Lemma 5.5.7]
,
\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
is a Morse–Bott function. Let
x,y\in\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma})
. As
\Sigma
is simply connected by assumption, there exists some path
\gamma
from
x
to
y
in
\Sigma
and a homotopy from
\gamma
to
\varphi\circ\gamma
with fixed endpoints. Extend this homotopy to a path in
\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}\Sigma
from
c_{x}
to
c_{y}
. Choose a Morse function
h
on
\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma})
and any critical point
c_{x}\in\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma})
. Then we can define a
\mathbb{Z}
-grading of
\RFC^{\varphi}(\Sigma,M)
by

\mu((c_{y},0),(c_{x},0))+\ind_{h}(c_{y})=\ind_{h}(c_{y})\hskip 22.99988pt\forall c_{y}\in\Crit(h),

and consequently,

\RFH^{\varphi}_{\ast}(\Sigma,M)=\HM_{\ast}(\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma});\mathbb{Z}_{2})\cong\operatorname{H}_{\ast}(\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma});\mathbb{Z}_{2})

as there are only twisted negative gradient flow lines with zero cascades, that is, ordinary Morse gradient flow lines of
h
. Indeed, suppose that there is a nonconstant twisted negative gradient flow line
(u,\tau)
of
\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
with asymptotics
(\gamma^{\pm},\tau^{\pm})
. Using the twisted negative gradient flow equation we estimate

\tau^{-}-\tau^{+}=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\mathinner{\!\bigl\lVert\grad_{J}\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(u(s),\tau(s))}\bigr\rVert}^{2}_{J}ds>0.

Hence
\tau^{+}<\tau^{-}
, contradicting
\tau^{\pm}=0
. ∎

3.4
Invariance of Twisted Rabinowitz–Floer Homology Under Twisted Homotopies of Liouville Domains

Definition 3.17 (Twisted Homotopy of Liouville Domains).

Given the completion
(M,\lambda)
of a Liouville domain
(W_{0},\lambda)
and
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
, a
twisted homotopy of Liouville domains in
M
is a time-dependent Hamiltonian function
H\in C^{\infty}(M\times I)
such that

(i)

W_{\sigma}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=H^{-1}_{\sigma}(\mathinner{(-\infty,0]})
is a Liouville domain with symplectic form
d\lambda|_{W_{\sigma}}
and boundary
\Sigma_{\sigma}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=H^{-1}_{\sigma}(0)
for all
\sigma\in I
,

(ii)

H_{\sigma}\in\mathscr{F}_{\varphi}(\Sigma_{\sigma})
for all
\sigma\in I
,

(iii)

\Sigma_{\sigma}\cap\supp f_{\varphi}=\varnothing
for all
\sigma\in I
.

We write
(H_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in I}
for a twisted homotopy of Liouville domains.

Theorem 3.2 (Invariance of Twisted Rabinowitz–Floer Homology).

If
(H_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in I}
is a twisted homotopy of Liouville domains such that both
\mathscr{A}^{H_{0}}_{\varphi}
and
\mathscr{A}^{H_{1}}_{\varphi}
are Morse–Bott, then there is a canonical isomorphism

\RFH^{\varphi}(\Sigma_{0},M)\cong\RFH^{\varphi}(\Sigma_{1},M).

Proof.

The proof follows from the same adiabatic argument as in
[
CF09
, p. 275–277]
. Crucial is that
[
CF09
, Theorem 3.6]
remains true in our setting, as well as the genericness of the Morse–Bott condition. Indeed, if
(M,\lambda)
is an exact symplectic manifold and
\varphi\in\Diff(M)
is of finite order such that
\varphi^{*}\lambda=\lambda
, then we have the following generalisation of
[
CF09
, Theorem B.1]
. Adapting the proof accordingly, one can show that there exists a subset

\mathscr{U}\subseteq\{H\in C^{\infty}_{\varphi}(M)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\supp dH\text{ compact}\},

of the second category such that for every
H\in\mathscr{U}
,
\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
is Morse–Bott with critical manifold being
\Fix(\varphi|_{H^{-1}(0)})
together with a disjoint union of circles. Again, this works only since the contact condition is an open condition. ∎

Remark 3.8.

Invariance of twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology allows us to define twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology also in the case where
\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
is not necessarily Morse–Bott. Indeed, as the proof of Theorem
3.2
shows, we can perturb the hypersurface
\Sigma
slightly to make it Morse–Bott. Thus we can define the twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology of such a hypersurface to be the twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology of any Morse–Bott perturbation. By Theorem
3.2
, this is indeed well-defined.

Corollary 3.5 (Independence).

Let
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
and
H_{0},H_{1}\in\mathscr{F}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)
be such that either
\mathscr{A}^{H_{0}}_{\varphi}
or
\mathscr{A}^{H_{1}}_{\varphi}
is Morse–Bott. Then the definition of twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology
\RFH^{\varphi}(\Sigma,M)
is independent of the choice of twisted defining Hamiltonian function for
\Sigma
.

Proof.

Note that
\mathscr{F}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)
is a convex space. Indeed, set

H_{\sigma}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(1-\sigma)H_{0}+\sigma H_{1}\hskip 22.99988pt\sigma\in I.

Then
\varphi^{*}H_{\sigma}=H_{\sigma}
,
dH_{\sigma}
has compact support and
X_{H_{\sigma}}|_{\Sigma}=R
for all
\sigma\in I
. Moreover, for the Liouville vector field
X\in\mathfrak{X}(M)
we compute

\frac{d}{dt}\bigg|_{t=0}H\circ\phi^{X}_{t}|_{\Sigma}=dH(X)|_{\Sigma}=d\lambda(X,X_{H})|_{\Sigma}=\lambda(X_{H})|_{\Sigma}=\lambda(R)=1,

for any
H\in\mathscr{F}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)
, and thus
H<0
on
\Int W
and
H>0
on
M\setminus W
. Consequently,
H^{-1}_{\sigma}(0)=\Sigma
and so
H_{\sigma}\in\mathscr{F}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)
for all
\sigma\in I
. Hence
(H_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in I}
is a twisted homotopy of Liouville domains in
M
and Theorem
3.2
implies the claim.
∎

3.5
Twisted Leaf-Wise Intersection Points

Definition 3.18 (Twisted Leaf-Wise Intersection Point).

Let
(M,\lambda)
be the completion of a Liouville domain
(W,\lambda)
and let
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
be a Liouville automorphism. A point
x\in\Sigma
is a
twisted leaf-wise intersection point
for a Hamiltonian symplectomorphism
\varphi_{F}\in\Ham(M,d\lambda)
, if

\varphi_{F}(x)\in L_{\varphi(x)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\{\phi^{R}_{t}(\varphi(x))\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}t\in\mathbb{R}\}}.

Definition 3.19 (Twisted Moser Pair).

Let
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
. A
twisted Moser pair
is defined to be a tuple
\mathfrak{M}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=(\chi H,F)
, where

(i)

H\in C^{\infty}_{\varphi}(M)
,
F\in C^{\infty}_{\varphi}(M\times\mathbb{R})
and
\chi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{1},I)
such that
\int_{0}^{1}\chi=1
. Any time-dependent Hamiltonian function
\chi H
is said to be
weakly time-dependent
.

(ii)

\supp\chi\subseteq\mathinner{\bigl(0,\frac{1}{2}\bigr)}
and
F_{t}=0
for all
t\in\mathinner{\bigl[0,\frac{1}{2}\bigr]}
.

Lemma 3.9.

Let
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
. For all
H\in\mathscr{F}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)
and
\varphi_{F}\in\Ham(M,d\lambda)
there exists a corresponding twisted Moser pair
\mathfrak{M}
such that the flow of
\chi X_{H}
is a time-reparametrisation of the flow of
X_{H}
.

Proof.

For constructing the Hamiltonian perturbation
\widetilde{F}
, fix
\rho\in C^{\infty}(I,I)
such that

\rho(t)=\begin{cases}0&t\in\mathinner{\bigl[0,\frac{1}{2}\bigr]},\\
1&t\in\mathinner{\bigl[\frac{2}{3},1\bigr]}.\end{cases}

See Figure
9(a)
. Then define
\widetilde{F}\in C^{\infty}_{\varphi}(M\times\mathbb{R})
by

\widetilde{F}(x,t)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\dot{\rho}(t-k)F\mathinner{\bigl(\varphi^{-k}(x),\rho(t-k)\bigr)}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall t\in\mathinner{[k,k+1]},

for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. See Figure
9(b)
. Then
\widetilde{F}_{t}=0
for all
t\in\mathinner{\bigl[0,\frac{1}{2}\bigr]}
, and

\phi^{X_{\widetilde{F}}}_{t}=\phi^{X_{F}}_{\rho(t)}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall t\in I.

Indeed, we compute

\frac{d}{dt}\phi^{X_{F}}_{\rho(t)}=\dot{\rho}(t)\frac{d}{d\rho}\phi^{X_{F}}_{\rho(t)}=\dot{\rho}(t)\mathinner{\bigl(X_{F_{\rho(t)}}\circ\phi^{X_{F}}_{\rho(t)}\bigr)}=X_{\widetilde{F}_{t}}\circ\phi^{X_{F}}_{\rho(t)}.

In particular

\varphi_{\widetilde{F}}=\phi^{X_{\widetilde{F}}}_{1}=\phi^{X_{F}}_{\rho(1)}=\phi^{X_{F}}_{1}=\varphi_{F}.

Finally, we have that

\phi^{\chi X_{H}}_{t}=\phi^{X_{H}}_{\tau(t)}\hskip 22.99988pt\text{with}\hskip 22.99988pt\tau(t)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\int_{0}^{t}\chi,

as we compute

\frac{d}{dt}\phi^{X_{H}}_{\tau(t)}=\chi(t)\frac{d}{d\tau}\phi^{X_{H}}_{\tau(t)}=\chi(t)X_{H}\circ\phi^{X_{H}}_{\tau(t)},

and thus we conclude by the uniqueness of integral curves.
∎

(a)

The smooth function
\rho
.

(b)

The derivative
\dot{\rho}
of
\rho
.

Figure 9
:

Lemma 3.10.

Let
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
and
\varphi_{F}\in\Ham(M,d\lambda)
a Hamiltonian symplectomorphism. If
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Crit\mathscr{A}^{\mathfrak{M}}_{\varphi}
, then
x\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\gamma\mathinner{\bigl(\frac{1}{2}\bigr)}
is a twisted leaf-wise intersection point for
\varphi_{F}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{M}=(\chi H,F)
denote the twisted Moser pair from Lemma
3.9
. Using Proposition
3.1
we compute

\displaystyle\partial_{t}H(\gamma(t))

\displaystyle=dH(\dot{\gamma}(t))

\displaystyle=dH(\tau X_{\chi(t)H}(\gamma(t))+X_{F_{t}}(\gamma(t)))

\displaystyle=dH(\tau\chi(t)X_{H}(\gamma(t)))

\displaystyle=\tau\chi(t)dH(X_{H}(\gamma(t)))

\displaystyle=0

for all
t\in\mathinner{\bigl[0,\frac{1}{2}\bigr]}
. Thus
H\circ\gamma=c\in\mathbb{R}
on
\mathinner{\bigl[0,\frac{1}{2}\bigr]}
with

0=\int_{0}^{1}\chi H(\gamma)=\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi H(\gamma)=c\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi=c\int_{0}^{1}\chi=c.

Consequently,
\gamma(0)\in L_{x}
and
x\in\Sigma
. Moreover, also
\gamma(1)=\varphi(\gamma(0))\in\Sigma
by the
\varphi
-invariance of
H
. For
t\in\mathinner{\bigl[\frac{1}{2},1\bigr]}
,
\dot{\gamma}=X_{F_{t}}(\gamma)
and so
\gamma(1)=\varphi_{F}(x)\in\Sigma
. We conclude

L_{\varphi(x)}=\{\phi^{R}_{t}(\varphi(x))\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}t\in\mathbb{R}\}=\{\varphi(\phi^{R}_{t}(x))\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}t\in\mathbb{R}\}=\varphi(L_{x}),

and so
\varphi_{F}(x)=\gamma(1)=\varphi(\gamma(0))\in L_{\varphi(x)}
.
∎

Theorem 3.3.

Let
(W,\lambda)
be a Liouville domain with displaceable boundary in the completion
(M,\lambda)
and
\varphi\in\Aut(W,\lambda)
. Then
\RFH^{\varphi}(\Sigma,M)\cong 0
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\Sigma=\partial W
is displaceable in
M
via
\varphi_{F}\in\Ham_{c}(M,d\lambda)
and choose Rabinowitz–Floer data
(H,J)
for
\varphi
. Denote by
\mathfrak{M}=(\chi H,F)
the associated twisted Moser pair from Lemma
3.9
. Then
\Crit\mathscr{A}^{\mathfrak{M}}_{\varphi}=\varnothing
. Indeed, if there exists
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Crit\mathscr{A}^{\mathfrak{M}}_{\varphi}
, then
\gamma\mathinner{\bigl(\frac{1}{2}\bigr)}
is a twisted leaf-wise intersection point for
\varphi_{F}
by Lemma
3.10
. However, this is impossible as by displaceability we have that
\varphi_{F}(\Sigma)\cap\Sigma=\varnothing
. Consequently, the perturbed twisted Rabinowitz action functional
\mathscr{A}^{\mathfrak{M}}_{\varphi}
is a Morse function. By adapting the Fundamental Lemma to the current setting as in
[
AF10
, Theorem 2.9]
, the Floer homology
\HF(\mathscr{A}^{\mathfrak{M}}_{\varphi})
is well-defined. By making use of continuation homomorphisms we have that

0=\HF(\mathscr{A}^{\mathfrak{M}}_{\varphi})\cong\HF(\mathscr{A}^{(\chi H,0)}_{\varphi})\cong\RFH^{\varphi}(\Sigma,M),

where the last equation is the observation that twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology in the autonomous case extends to the weakly time-dependent case without any issues. Crucial is, that the period–action equality (see Remark
3.5
) is still valid. Indeed, we compute

\mathscr{A}^{(\chi H,0)}_{\varphi}(\gamma,\tau)=\int_{0}^{1}\gamma^{*}\lambda=\int_{0}^{1}\lambda(\dot{\gamma})=\tau\int_{0}^{1}\chi\lambda(R(\gamma))=\tau\int_{0}^{1}\chi=\tau

for all
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Crit\mathscr{A}^{(\chi H,0)}_{\varphi}
.
∎

4
Applications

In this chapter we give two applications of the abstract machinery developed in the previous section and prove Theorem
1.1
as well as Theorem
1.5
(see Theorem
4.3
).

4.1
Existence of Noncontractible Periodic Reeb Orbits

We define an equivariant version of twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology for the discrete group
\mathbb{Z}_{m}
following
[
AF12
, p. 487]
. In general, suppose that a topological manifold
M
admits a group action by a topological group
G
. Then there exists a principal
G
-bundle
EG\to BG
, where
BG=EG/G
denotes the classifying space of
G
and
EG
is weakly contractible. Then
G
acts freely on
EG\times M
via the diagonal action. Thus we can define the
G
-equivariant homology of
M
by

\operatorname{H}_{\ast}^{G}(M;R)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\operatorname{H}_{\ast}(EG\times M/G;R),

for any coefficient ring
R
and where
EG\times M/G
is the homotopy quotient of
M
by
G
. See
[
Tu20
, p. 30–31]
. For example, if
G=\mathbb{Z}_{m}
, then
E\mathbb{Z}_{m}=\mathbb{S}^{\infty}
and
B\mathbb{Z}_{m}=\mathbb{S}^{\infty}/\mathbb{Z}_{m}
is a lens space. Since
\mathbb{Z}_{m}
acts freely on
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}
, there is a fibre bundle

EG\to EG\times M/G\to M/G,

inducing an isomorphism

\operatorname{H}_{*}^{G}(M)=\operatorname{H}_{*}(M/G)

by
[
Tu20
, Corollary 9.6]
and
[
Tu20
, Theorem 3.3]
. This observation will be crucial in widehat follows. Explicitly, consider the free smooth discrete action on the odd-dimensional sphere generated by the rotation
\varphi
from Example
2.13
. Define a twisted defining Hamiltonian function
H\in\mathscr{F}_{\varphi}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1})
by

H(z)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{2}\mathinner{\bigl(\beta(\mathinner{\lvert z\rvert}^{2})-1\bigr)}

for some sufficiently small mollification of the piecewise linear function

\beta(r)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}&r\in\mathinner{\bigl(-\infty,\frac{1}{2}\bigr]},\\
r&r\in\mathinner{\bigl[\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}\bigr]},\\
\frac{3}{2}&r\in\mathinner{\bigl[\frac{3}{2},+\infty\bigr)}.\end{cases}

Fix a
\varphi
-invariant
\omega
-compatible almost complex structure on
(\mathbb{C}^{n},\lambda)
, where
\lambda
is given by (
10
). Then the rotation
\varphi
induces a free
\mathbb{Z}_{m}
-action on
\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
and on the moduli space of twisted negative gradient flow lines with cascades of
\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
. Therefore, we can define the
\mathbb{Z}_{m}
-equivariant twisted Rabinowitz-Floer homology

\overline{\RFH}^{\varphi}_{k}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{m})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\ker\overline{\partial}_{k}}{\im\overline{\partial}_{k+1}}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall k\in\mathbb{Z},

as the homology of the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded chain complex (see Remark
3.7
)

\overline{\partial}_{k}\colon\RFC^{\varphi}_{k}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\mathbb{C}^{n})/\mathbb{Z}_{m}\to\RFC^{\varphi}_{k-1}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\mathbb{C}^{n})/\mathbb{Z}_{m}

given by

\overline{\partial}_{k}[(\gamma,\tau)]\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=[\partial_{k}(\gamma,\tau)]\hskip 22.99988pt(\gamma,\tau)\in\Crit h,

for some
\varphi
-invariant Morse function
h
on
\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
. More generally, if
G
is a finite symmetry of a Hamiltonian system which acts free on the displaceable regular energy hypersurface, one can define the
G
-equivariant twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology as above if the twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology is defined. Under some mild index assumption on the Conley–Zehnder index, the resulting
G
-equivariant twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology is isomorphic to the Tate homology of
G
with coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. See
[
Ruc22
, Theorem 5.6]
for a proof and
[
Wei94
, Definition 6.2.4]
as well as
[
Bro82
, p. 135]
for a definition of Tate homology.

Theorem 4.1.

Let
n\geq 2
. For
m\geq 1
consider the rotation

\varphi\colon\mathbb{C}^{n}\to\mathbb{C}^{n},\hskip 11.49994pt\varphi(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\bigl(e^{2\pi ik_{1}/m}z_{1},\dots,e^{2\pi ik_{n}/m}z_{n}\bigr)}

for
k_{1},\dots,k_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}
coprime to
m
. Then

\overline{\RFH}^{\varphi}_{k}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{m})\cong\begin{cases}\mathbb{Z}_{2}&m\text{ even},\\
0&m\text{ odd},\end{cases}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall k\in\mathbb{Z},

If
m
is even, then
\overline{\RFH}^{\varphi}_{k}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{m})
is generated by a noncontractible periodic Reeb orbit in the lens space
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{m}
for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

First we consider the special case

\varphi\colon\mathbb{C}^{n}\to\mathbb{C}^{n},\hskip 22.99988pt\varphi(z)=e^{2\pi i/m}z.

The hypersurface
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}\subseteq(\mathbb{C}^{n},\lambda)
is of restricted contact type with contact form
\lambda|_{\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}}
and associated Reeb vector field

R=2\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl(y_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}-x_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}\biggr)}\bigg|_{\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}}=2i\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathinner{\biggl(\overline{z}_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{j}}-z_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{j}}\biggr)}\bigg|_{\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}}.

Suppose
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
. If
\tau=0
, then
\gamma
is constant. This cannot happen as
\Fix(\varphi|_{\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}})=\varnothing
. So we assume
\tau\neq 0
. Define a reparametrisation

\gamma_{\tau}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\hskip 22.99988pt\gamma_{\tau}(t)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\gamma(t/\tau).

Then
\gamma_{\tau}
is the unique integral curve of
R
starting at
z\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\gamma(0)
and thus

\gamma_{\tau}(t)=e^{-2it}z\hskip 22.99988pt\forall t\in\mathbb{R}.

From
\gamma(t)=\gamma_{\tau}(\tau t)
and the requirement

e^{-2i\tau}z=\gamma(1)=\varphi(\gamma(0))=\varphi(z)=e^{2\pi i/m}z,

we conclude
\tau\in\frac{\pi}{m}(m\mathbb{Z}-1)
. Hence

\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}=\mathinner{\bigl\{\mathinner{\bigl(\phi^{\tau_{k}R}(z),\tau_{k}\bigr)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}k\in\mathbb{Z},z\in\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}\bigr\}}\cong\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}\times\mathbb{Z},

for any
H\in\mathscr{F}_{\varphi}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1})
, where we define
\tau_{k}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\pi}{m}(mk-1)
. By Proposition
3.2
,
(z_{0},\eta)\in T_{z}\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}\times\mathbb{R}
belongs to the kernel of the Hessian at
(z,k)\in\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
if and only if
\eta=0
and

z_{0}\in\ker\mathinner{\bigl(D(\phi^{R}_{-\tau_{k}}\circ\varphi)|_{z}-\id_{T_{z}\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}}\bigr)}.

A direct computation yields
D(\phi^{R}_{-\tau_{k}}\circ\varphi)|_{z}=\id_{T_{z}\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}}
and thus the twisted Rabinowitz action functional
\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
is Morse–Bott with spheres.

Figure 10
:
The critical manifold
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}\times\mathbb{Z}
with the standard height function, the Morse–Bott function
f
and the resulting chain complex.

The full Conley–Zehnder index
[
FK18
, Definition 10.4.1]
gives rise to a locally constant function

\widehat{\mu}_{\mathrm{CZ}}\colon\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}\to\mathbb{Z},\hskip 22.99988pt\widehat{\mu}(z,k)=(2k-1)n.

Note that the definition of the Conley–Zehnder index also applies in this degenerate case, compare
[
FK18
, Remark 10.4.2]
. By the adapted proof of the Hofer–Wysocki–Zehnder Theorem
[
FK18
, Theorem 12.2.1]
to the
n
-dimensional setting, the full Conley–Zehnder index coincides with the transverse Conley–Zehnder index
\mu_{\CZ}
. Indeed, for a critical point
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
define a smooth path

\Psi\colon I\to\Sp(n),\hskip 22.99988pt\Psi_{t}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=D\phi^{H}_{\tau t}|_{\gamma(0)}\colon\mathbb{C}^{n}\to\mathbb{C}^{n}.

Adapting the proof of
[
FK18
, Lemma 12.2.3 (iii)]
, we get that

\Psi_{1}(R(\gamma(0)))=R(\gamma(1))\hskip 22.99988pt\text{and}\hskip 22.99988pt\Psi_{1}(\gamma(0))=\gamma(1).

Arguing as in
[
FK18
, p. 235–236]
we conclude

\mu_{\CZ}(\gamma,\tau)=\widehat{\mu}_{\CZ}(\gamma,\tau).

Fix
z_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}
and define
\eta\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\phi^{\tau_{0}R}(z_{0})
. Note that
\phi^{\tau_{k}R}(z)
belongs to the same equivalence class in
\pi_{0}\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}
as
\eta
for all
z\in\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
because
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}
is simply connected for
n\geq 2
. Let
h\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1})
be the standard height function. By Remark
3.7
,
\RFH^{\varphi}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\mathbb{C}^{n})
carries the
\mathbb{Z}
-grading

\mu((z,k),(z_{0},0))+\ind_{h}(z)=2kn+\ind_{h}(z)\hskip 22.99988pt\forall(z,k)\in\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}\times\mathbb{Z}.

We claim that the number of twisted negative gradient flow lines between the minimum of
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}\times\{k+1\}
and the maximum of
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}\times\{k\}
must be odd, so that the critical manifold
\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
looks like a string of pearls, see Figure
10
. Indeed, if there is an even number of such negative gradient flow lines, then
\RFH^{\varphi}_{\ast}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\mathbb{C}^{n})\neq 0
, contradicting Theorem
3.3
as
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}
is displaceable in the completion
\mathbb{C}^{n}
. To compute the
\mathbb{Z}_{m}
-equivariant twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology, choose the additional
\mathbb{Z}_{m}
-invariant Morse–Bott function
f
from Example
2.1
. Additionaly, choose a
\mathbb{Z}_{m}
-invariant Morse function on
\Crit f
. For example, one can take

h\colon\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{R},\hskip 22.99988pth(t)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\cos(2\pi mt).

The resulting chain complex is given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{m}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{m}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{m}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{m}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}^{m}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{1}}

where
\mathbbm{1}\in M_{m\times m}(\mathbb{Z}_{2})
has every entry equal to
1
and
A\in M_{m\times m}(\mathbb{Z}_{2})
is defined by

A\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=I_{m\times m}+\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}e_{(j+1)j}+e_{1m},

where
e_{ij}\in M_{m\times m}(\mathbb{Z}_{2})
satisfies
(e_{ij})_{kl}=\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}
. Thus the resulting chain complex looks like a rope ladder. Compare Figure
10
. Passing to the quotient via the free
\mathbb{Z}_{m}
-action, we get the acyclic chain complex

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

if
m
is even and the alternating chain complex

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}

if
m
is odd.

For the general case, we note that
\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
is a disjoint union of spheres and different copies of
\mathbb{Z}
. It is therefore rather hard to compute the equivariant twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology directly via analysing the critical manifold. By Theorem
3.3
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\RFH^{\varphi}_{*}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\mathbb{C}^{n})\cong\RFH_{*}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\mathbb{C}^{n})

inducing a canonical isomorphism

\overline{\RFH}^{\varphi}_{*}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{m})\cong\RFH^{\mathbb{Z}_{m}}_{*}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\mathbb{C}^{n}),

where
\RFH^{\mathbb{Z}_{m}}_{*}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\mathbb{C}^{n})
denotes the
\mathbb{Z}_{m}
-equivariant Rabinowitz–Floer homology constructed in
[
AF12
, p. 487]
. A computation similar as before shows

\RFH^{\mathbb{Z}_{m}}_{k}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\mathbb{C}^{n})\cong\begin{cases}\mathbb{Z}_{2}&m\text{ even},\\
0&m\text{ odd},\end{cases}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}.

The crucial observation is, that
\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}\cong\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}\times\mathbb{Z}
. In particular, the string of pearls looks like in Figure
11
.

Lastly,
\overline{\RFH}^{\varphi}_{k}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{m})
is generated by a noncontractible periodic Reeb orbit in
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{m}
for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}
by Lemma
1.1
.
∎

Figure 11
:
The critical manifold
\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
together with the standard height function.

Remark 4.1 (Coefficients).

As
\overline{\RFH}^{\varphi}_{*}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{m})
vanishes for odd
m
, one should rather consider twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology with coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}
in this case. Using the polyfold approach, it might be possible to invoke
[
HWZ21
, Chapter 6]
to define coherent orientations on the moduli spaces. However, Lagrangian Floer homology admits an abstract polyfold description, but it is not always possible to define coherent orientations.

Using Theorem
4.1
we can finally prove Theorem
1.1
.

of Theorem
1.1
.

By assumption,
\Sigma
bounds a star-shaped domain
D
with respect to the origin. Thus
(D\cup\Sigma,\lambda)
is a Liouville domain with
\lambda
given by (
10
). By rescaling we may assume that
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}\subseteq D
. Define a smooth function

\delta\colon\Sigma\to\mathinner{(-\infty,0)}

by requiring
\delta(x)
to be the unique number such that
\phi^{X}_{\delta(x)}(x)\in\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}
,
x\in\Sigma
, where
X\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathbb{C}^{n})
denotes the Liouville vector field (
11
). We claim that
\delta\circ\varphi=\delta
. Indeed,
\delta(\varphi(x))
is the unique number such that
\phi^{X}_{\delta(\varphi(x))}(\varphi(x))\in\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}
. As the flow of
X
and
\varphi
commute by the proof of Lemma
3.6
, we conclude that
\phi^{X}_{\delta(\varphi(x))}(x)\in\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}
. Define a smooth family of star-shaped hypersurfaces
(\Sigma_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in I}

\Sigma_{\sigma}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\bigl\{\phi^{X}_{\sigma\delta(x)}(x)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in\Sigma\bigr\}}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{n}.

Then we compute

\displaystyle\varphi(\Sigma_{\sigma})

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\bigl\{\varphi\mathinner{\bigl(\phi^{X}_{\sigma\delta(x)}(x)\bigr)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in\Sigma\bigr\}}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\bigl\{\phi^{X}_{\sigma\delta(x)}(\varphi(x))\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in\Sigma\bigr\}}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\bigl\{\phi^{X}_{\sigma\delta(\varphi(x))}(\varphi(x))\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}x\in\Sigma\bigr\}}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\bigl\{\phi^{X}_{\sigma\delta(y)}(y)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}y\in\varphi(\Sigma)\bigr\}}

\displaystyle=\mathinner{\bigl\{\phi^{X}_{\sigma\delta(y)}(y)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}y\in\Sigma\bigr\}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{\sigma}

for all
\sigma\in I
and therefore we can find a twisted homotopy
(H_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in I}
of Liouville domains in
\mathbb{C}^{n}
. By Theorem
3.2
we have that

\RFH^{\varphi}_{*}(\Sigma,\mathbb{C}^{n})\cong\RFH_{*}^{\varphi}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\mathbb{C}^{n}),

giving rise to a canonical isomorphism of the associated
\mathbb{Z}_{m}
-equivariant twisted Rabinowitz–Floer homology

\overline{\RFH}^{\varphi}_{*}(\Sigma/\mathbb{Z}_{m})\cong\overline{\RFH}^{\varphi}_{*}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{m}).

However, by Theorem
4.1
the latter does not vanish as
m\geq 2
is even.
∎

Using Theorem
4.1
it is also possible to generalise
[
AF12
, Theorem 1.2]
. Define the set of
\varphi
-invariant Hamiltonian symplectomorphisms
by

\Ham^{\varphi}(\mathbb{C}^{n},d\lambda)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\{\varphi_{F}\in\Ham(\mathbb{C}^{n},d\lambda)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F_{t}(x)=F_{t}(\varphi(x))\>\forall(x,t)\in\mathbb{C}^{n}\times I\}.

If
\varphi_{F}\in\Ham^{\varphi}(\mathbb{C}^{n},d\lambda)
, then
\varphi\circ\varphi_{F}=\varphi_{F}\circ\varphi
. In particular
0\in\Fix(\varphi_{F})
, and thus no element in
\Ham^{\varphi}(\mathbb{C}^{n},d\lambda)
can displace a star-shaped hypersurface with respect to the origin in
\mathbb{C}^{n}
. We have the following result.

Theorem 4.2.

Let
\Sigma\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{n}
be a compact connected star-shaped hypersurface invariant under the rotation
\varphi
. Every element in
\Ham^{\varphi}(\mathbb{C}^{n},d\lambda)
admits infinitely many leaf-wise intersection points on
\Sigma
or there does exist a leaf-wise intersection point on a closed leaf.

Proof.

We reproduce the proof in
[
AF12
]
for completeness with minor modifications. Let
\varphi_{F}\in\Ham_{c}^{\varphi}(\mathbb{C}^{n},d\lambda)
and for
r\in\mathinner{[0,1]}
consider the smooth family of perturbed Rabinowitz action functionals

\mathscr{A}_{r}\colon\mathscr{L}\mathbb{C}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}

defined by

\mathscr{A}_{r}(\gamma,\tau)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\int_{0}^{1}\gamma^{*}\lambda-\tau\int_{0}^{1}H_{r}(\gamma(t))dt-r\int_{0}^{1}F_{t}(\gamma(t))dt,

where
(H_{r})_{r\in I}
is a twisted homotopy of Liouville domains from
\mathbb{S}^{2n-1}
to
\Sigma
. Clearly, every
\mathscr{A}_{r}
is
\varphi
-invariant. As in
[
AF10a
, Definition 5.1]
, we define the spectral value
\sigma([\xi])
of a homology class
[\xi]\in\RFH^{\mathbb{Z}_{m}}_{\ast}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\mathbb{C}^{n})
by

\sigma([\xi])\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\inf_{\eta\in[\xi]}\max_{\eta_{(\gamma,\tau)}\neq 0}\mathscr{A}_{0}(\gamma,\tau)\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}.

Moreover, we define the set

\mathfrak{S}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\{\sigma([\xi])\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}[\xi]\in\RFH^{\mathbb{Z}_{m}}_{\ast}(\mathbb{S}^{2n-1},\mathbb{C}^{n})\}}.

By Theorem
4.1
, we conclude that
\mathfrak{S}=2\pi\mathbb{Z}
. Hence
\mathscr{A}_{0}
has critical values of arbitrarily large critical value and so does
\mathscr{A}_{1}
by
[
AF10a
, Corollary 5.14]
. Thus
\mathscr{A}_{1}
has infinitely many critical points which give rise to leaf-wise intersection points by Lemma
3.10
. The map

\Crit\mathscr{A}_{1}\to\mathinner{\{\text{leaf-wise intersection points}\}}

is injective unless there exists a leaf-wise intersection point on a closed leaf. For the general case, use cut-off functions.
∎

4.2
A Forcing Theorem for Twisted Periodic Reeb Orbits

Definition 4.1 (Twisted Stable Hypersurface).

Let
(\Sigma,\omega|_{\Sigma},\lambda)
be a stable hypersurface in a connected symplectic manifold
(M,\omega)
and
\varphi\in\Symp(M,\omega)
. We say that
\Sigma
is
twisted by
\varphi
, if
\varphi(\Sigma)=\Sigma
,
\varphi
is of finite order and
\varphi^{*}\lambda=\lambda
.

Example 4.1.

Consider the stable hypersurface
\Sigma_{c}\subseteq(T^{*}\mathbb{T}^{n},\omega_{\sigma},H)
for
c>0
as in Example
2.7
. Let
\varphi\in\Diff(\mathbb{T}^{n})
be an isometry of finite order such that

D\varphi\circ J=J\circ D\varphi

(33)

holds and consider the cotangent lift (
2.6
)

D\varphi^{\dagger}\colon\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{T}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n},\hskip 22.99988ptD\varphi^{\dagger}(q,p)=\mathinner{\bigl(\varphi(q),\mathinner{\bigl(D\varphi^{-1}(q)\bigr)}^{t}p\bigr)}.

Then clearly
\varphi(\Sigma_{c})=\Sigma_{c}
as
\varphi
is an isometry and
D\varphi^{\dagger}
is of finite order as
\varphi
is. Moreover,
D\varphi^{\dagger}\in\Symp(T^{*}\mathbb{T}^{n},\omega_{\sigma})
, as
D\varphi^{\dagger}\in\Symp(T^{*}\mathbb{T}^{n},\omega_{0})
by Proposition
2.1
and
D\varphi^{\dagger}
preserves
\sigma
by assumption (
33
). Lastly, we have that
\varphi^{*}\lambda=\lambda
as one sees by considering the formula (
8
) together with assumption (
33
).

Let
(\Sigma,\omega|_{\Sigma},\lambda)
be a twisted stable hypersurface for
\varphi\in\Symp(M,\omega)
in a connected symplectically aspherical symplectic manifold
(M,\omega)
, that is,
[\omega]|_{\pi_{2}(M)}=0
. As
\varphi
is of finite order by assumption, we can define the
set of twisted contractible loops
, written
\Lambda_{\varphi}M\subseteq\Lambda M
, as follows. We say that a contractible free loop
v\in\Lambda M
is in
\Lambda_{\varphi}M
, if there exists
\gamma\in\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}M
such that

v(t)=\gamma(mt)\hskip 22.99988pt\forall t\in\mathbb{T},

where
m\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\ord\varphi
. Then we can define a generalisation of the twisted Rabinowitz action functional

\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}\colon\Lambda_{\varphi}M\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R},\hskip 11.49994pt\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(v,\tau)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{m}\int_{\mathbb{D}}\overline{v}^{*}\omega-\tau\int_{0}^{1}H(v(t))dt,

(34)

where
\overline{v}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},M)
is a filling of
v
and
H
is any twisted defining Hamiltonian function for
\Sigma
. Then
(v,\tau)\in\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
if and only if
(\gamma,\tau)\in\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}\Sigma
solves

\dot{\gamma}(t)=\tau R(\gamma(t))\hskip 22.99988pt\forall t\in\mathbb{R},

where
R\in\mathfrak{X}(\Sigma)
denotes the stable Reeb vector field
2.11
. We call the projection of the set of critical points of
\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
to
\Lambda_{\varphi}M

contractible twisted closed characteristics
and denote it by
\mathscr{C}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)
. Define a function, called the
\omega
-energy
, by

\Omega\colon\mathscr{C}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)\to\mathbb{R},\hskip 22.99988pt\Omega(v)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{1}{m}\int_{\mathbb{D}}\overline{v}^{*}\omega.

It follows that
\Omega(v)=\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(v,\tau)
.

Example 4.2.

Consider the twisted stable hypersurface
\Sigma_{c}\subseteq(T^{*}\mathbb{T}^{n},\omega_{\sigma},H)
as in Example
4.1
. By adapting Example
2.8
, we have that
(q,p)\in\Sigma_{k}
gives rise to a contractible twisted closed characteristic if and only if

\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{sJ}pds+q=\varphi(q),\hskip 11.49994pte^{\tau J}p=\mathinner{\bigl(D\varphi^{-1}(q)\bigr)}^{t}p,\hskip 11.49994pt\text{and}\hskip 11.49994pt\mathinner{\lVert p\rVert}^{2}=2c.

A computation similar to
[
CFP10
, p. 1843]
shows

\Omega\colon\mathscr{C}_{\varphi}(\Sigma_{c})\to\mathbb{R},\hskip 22.99988pt\Omega(v)=c\tau.

In order to state the main result of this section, we need two additional preliminary definitions.

Definition 4.2 (Morse–Bott Component,
[
AF10
, p. 86]
).

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and
f\in C^{\infty}(M)
. A subset
C\subseteq\Crit f
is called a
Morse–Bott component
, if

(i)

C
is a connected embedded submanifold of
M
.

(ii)

T_{x}C=\ker\Hess f(x)
for all
x\in C
.

Example 4.3 (
[
AF10
, Lemma 2.12]
).

In the setting of Proposition
3.2
, any connected component of
\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma})\subseteq\Crit\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
is a Morse–Bott component. Indeed, we have that

\ker\Hess\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}|_{(x,0)}\cong\ker(D\varphi_{x}-\id_{T_{x}\Sigma})=T_{x}\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma})

for all
x\in\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma})
.

Definition 4.3 (
[
CFP10
, p. 1768]
).

A symplectic manifold
(M,\omega)
is called
geometrically bounded
, if there exists an
\omega
-compatible almost complex structure
J
and a complete Riemannian metric such that the following conditions hold.

(i)

There are constants
C_{0},C_{1}>0
with

\omega(Jv,v)\geq C_{0}\mathinner{\lVert v\rVert}^{2}\hskip 22.99988pt\text{and}\hskip 22.99988pt\mathinner{\lvert\omega(u,v)\rvert}\leq C_{1}\mathinner{\lVert u\rVert}\mathinner{\lVert v\rVert}

for all
u,v\in T_{x}M
and
x\in M
.

(ii)

The sectional curvature of the metric is bounded above, and its injectivity radius is bounded away from zero.

Example 4.4 (
[
CFP10
, p. 1768]
).

Twisted cotangent bundles are geometrically bounded.

Theorem 4.3 (Forcing).

Let
\Sigma
be a twisted stable displaceable hypersurface in a symplectically aspherical, geometrically bounded, symplectic manifold
(M,\omega)
for some
\varphi\in\Symp(M,\omega)
and suppose that
v^{-}\in\mathscr{C}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)
belongs to a Morse–Bott component
C
of the twisted Rabinowitz action functional (
34
). Then there exists a contractible twisted closed characteristic
v\in\mathscr{C}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)\setminus C
such that

\Omega(v)-\Omega(v^{-})\leq e(\Sigma).

Corollary 4.1.

Let
\Sigma
be a twisted stable displaceable hypersurface in a symplectically aspherical, geometrically bounded, symplectic manifold
(M,\omega)
for some symplectomorphism
\varphi\in\Symp(M,\omega)
. If
\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma})\neq\varnothing
, then there exists a contractible twisted closed characteristic
v\in\mathscr{C}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)\setminus\Fix(\varphi|_{\Sigma})
such that

\Omega(v)\leq e(\Sigma).

Corollary 4.2 (
[
MS17
, Theorem 12.3.4]
,
[
HZ94
, p. 171]
).

We have that

e(\overline{B}_{r}^{2n}(0))=\pi r^{2}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall r>0,

where
\overline{B}^{2n}_{r}(0)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2n}
denotes the closed ball around the origin of radius
r
.

Proof.

By monotonicity and
[
MS17
, Exercise 12.3.7]
we have that

e(\partial\overline{B}^{2n}_{r}(0))\leq e(\overline{B}^{2n}_{r}(0))\leq\pi r^{2}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall r>0.

The Reeb flow on
\partial\overline{B}^{2n}_{r}(0)
is given by

\phi^{R_{r}}_{t}(z)=e^{-2it/r^{2}}z\hskip 22.99988pt\forall z\in\partial\overline{B}^{2n}_{r}(0).

Hence the parametrised periodic Reeb orbits are
(\phi^{R_{r}}(z),\tau)
with
\tau\in\pi r^{2}\mathbb{Z}
. But Corollary
4.1
implies the existence of a nonconstant closed characteristic
v
on the hypersurface
\partial\overline{B}^{2n}_{r}(0)
such that

0<\tau=\Omega(v)\leq e(\partial\overline{B}^{2n}_{r}(0))\leq\pi r^{2}.

This is only possible for
\tau=\pi r^{2}
and the statement follows.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.3
.

This proof uses a method called a “homotopy of homotopies argument”. Fix
\varepsilon>0
and choose a Hamiltonian function
F\in C^{\infty}_{c}(M\times I)
satisfying

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert F\right\rVert}<e(\Sigma)+\varepsilon\hskip 22.99988pt\text{and}\hskip 22.99988pt\varphi_{F}(\Sigma)\cap\Sigma=\varnothing.

For an appropriate twisted defining Hamiltonian function
H
for
\Sigma
we denote by
\mathfrak{M}
the associated twisted Moser pair. The actual construction of
H
is very cumbersome and is carried out in
[
CFP10
]
. The crucial observation here is that
[
CFP10
, Proposition 2.6]
gives a
\varphi
-invariant stable tubular neighbourhood of
\Sigma
as
\varphi^{*}\lambda=\lambda
by invoking the equivariant Darboux–Weinstein Theorem
[
VG84
, Theorem 22.1]
. Moreover, we choose a smooth family
(\beta_{r})_{r\in\mathinner{[0,+\infty)}}
of cutoff functions
\beta_{r}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},I)
such that

\begin{cases}\beta_{r}(s)=0&\mathinner{\lvert s\rvert}\geq r,\\
\beta_{r}(s)=1&\mathinner{\lvert s\rvert}\leq r-1,\\
s\beta^{\prime}_{r}(s)\leq 0&\forall s\in\mathbb{R},\end{cases}

for all
r\in\mathinner{[0,+\infty)}
. Define a family of twisted Rabinowitz action functionals

\mathscr{A}_{r}\colon\Lambda_{\varphi}M\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}

by

\mathscr{A}_{r}(v,\tau,s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}(v,\tau)-\beta_{r}(s)\int_{0}^{1}F_{t}(v(t))dt

for all
r\in\mathinner{[0,+\infty)}
. Note that
\mathscr{A}_{0}=\mathscr{A}^{H}_{\varphi}
. For a suitable
\varphi
-invariant
\omega
-compatible almost complex structure we consider the moduli space

\mathscr{M}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\mathinner{\{(u,\tau,r)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},\mathscr{L}_{\varphi}M\times\mathbb{R})\times\mathinner{[0,+\infty)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}(u,\tau,r)\text{ solution of \eqref{eq:moduli_space}}\}},

where

\begin{cases}\partial_{s}(u,\tau)=\grad\mathscr{A}_{r}|_{(u(s),\tau(s),s)}&\forall s\in\mathbb{R},\\
\displaystyle\lim_{s\to-\infty}(u(s),\tau(s))=(v^{-},\tau^{-}),\\
\displaystyle\lim_{s\to+\infty}(u(s),\tau(s))\in C.\end{cases}

(35)

Note that always
(v^{-},\tau^{-},0)\in\mathscr{M}
. The proof is now based on the following observation. If

\Omega(v)>\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}+\Omega(v^{-})\hskip 22.99988pt\forall v\in\mathscr{C}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)\setminus C

(36)

holds, then
\mathscr{M}
is compact. This is absurd. Indeed, the moduli space
\mathscr{M}
is the zero level set of a Fredholm section of a bundle over a Banach manifold. As
v^{-}
belongs to a Morse–Bott component, the Fredholm section is regular at the point
v^{-}
, that is, the linearisation of the gradient flow equation is surjective there. By compactness, we can therefore perturb the Fredholm section to make it transverse. Hence
\mathscr{M}
is a compact smooth manifold with boundary consisting precisely of the point
v^{-}
. There do not exist such manifolds. Thus we conclude that there exists
v\in\mathscr{C}_{\varphi}(\Sigma)\setminus C
such that

\Omega(v)-\Omega(v^{-})\leq\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}<e(\Sigma)+\varepsilon.

As
\varepsilon>0
was arbitrary, the statement follows. We prove the compactness of
\mathscr{M}
under assumption (
36
) in four steps.

Step 1: If
(u,\tau,r)\in\mathscr{M}
, then
E(u,\tau)\leq\mathinner{\!\left\lVert F\right\rVert}
.
We estimate

\displaystyle E(u,\tau)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\mathinner{\lVert\partial_{s}(u,\tau)\rVert}^{2}ds

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}d\mathscr{A}_{r}(\partial_{s}(u,\tau),s)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\frac{d}{ds}\mathscr{A}_{r}(u,\tau,s)ds-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(\partial_{s}\mathscr{A}_{r})(u,\tau,s)ds

\displaystyle=\lim_{s\to+\infty}\mathscr{A}_{r}(u,\tau,s)-\lim_{s\to-\infty}\mathscr{A}_{r}(u,\tau,s)-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(\partial_{s}\mathscr{A}_{r})(u,\tau,s)ds

\displaystyle=\mathscr{A}_{0}(v^{+},\tau^{+})-\mathscr{A}_{0}(v^{-},\tau^{-})-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(\partial_{s}\mathscr{A}_{r})(u,\tau,s)ds

\displaystyle=-\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}(\partial_{s}\mathscr{A}_{r})(u,\tau,s)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\dot{\beta}_{r}(s)\int_{0}^{1}F_{t}(u(s,t))dtds

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}_{+}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\dot{\beta}_{r}(s)ds-\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}_{-}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\dot{\beta}_{r}(s)ds

\displaystyle=\beta_{r}(0)(\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}_{-}+\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}_{+})

\displaystyle=\beta_{r}(0)\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}

\displaystyle\leq\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert},

as
\mathscr{A}_{0}(v^{+},\tau^{+})=\mathscr{A}_{0}(v^{-},\tau^{-})
since
C
is connected.

Step 2: There exists
r_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
r\leq r_{0}
for all
(u,\tau,r)\in\mathscr{M}
.
Crucial is the existence of a constant
\delta>0
such that

\mathinner{\lVert\grad\mathscr{A}_{r}|_{(v,\tau,s)}\rVert}\geq\delta\hskip 22.99988pt\forall(v,\tau,s)\in\Lambda_{\varphi}M\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}.

This is proven along the lines of
[
CF09
, Lemma 3.9]
. With the above inequality and Step 1 we estimate

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert F\right\rVert}\geq E(u,\tau)\geq\int_{-r}^{r}\mathinner{\lVert\grad\mathscr{A}_{r}|_{(u(s),\tau(s),s)}\rVert}^{2}ds\geq 2r\delta^{2},

and thus we can set

r_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}=\frac{\mathinner{\!\left\lVert F\right\rVert}}{2\delta^{2}}.

Step 3: There exists
C>0
such that
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\tau\right\rVert}_{\infty}\leq C
for all
(u,\tau,r)\in\mathscr{M}
.
This is a delicate estimate based on the construction of the defining Hamiltonian
H
for
\Sigma
as well as an extension of the stablising form and proceeds as in
[
CFP10
]
. Particularly crucial is
[
CFP10
, Proposition 4.1]
.

Step 4: If (
36
) holds, then
\mathscr{M}
is compact.
Let
(u_{k},\tau_{k},r_{k})
be a sequence in the moduli space
\mathscr{M}
. By Step 2 and Step 3, the sequences
(r_{k})
and
(\tau_{k})
are uniformly bounded. Thus
(u_{k},\tau_{k},r_{k})
admits a
C^{\infty}_{\loc}
-convergent subsequence by standard arguments. Indeed, the uniform
L^{\infty}
-bound on the sequence
(u_{k})
follows from the assumption that
(M,\omega)
is geometrically bounded and the uniform
L^{\infty}
-bound on the derivatives
(Du_{k})
follows from Corollary
D.1
by the assumption that
(M,\omega)
is symplectically aspherical. Denote the limit of this subsequence by
(u,\tau,r)
. This limit clearly satisfies the first equation in (
35
), thus one only needs to check the asymptotic conditions in (
35
). Again by compactness,
(u,\tau)
converges to critical points
(w^{\pm},\tau^{\pm})
of
\mathscr{A}_{0}
at its asymptotic ends. We claim that

\mathscr{A}_{r}(u(s),\tau(s),s)\in\mathinner{[-\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}+\Omega(v^{-}),\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}+\Omega(v^{-})]}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall s\in\mathbb{R}.

(37)

In particular,
\Omega(w^{\pm})\in\mathinner{[-\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}+\Omega(v^{-}),\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}+\Omega(v^{-})]}
. So if (
37
) holds, then by assumption (
36
) we conclude
(w^{\pm},\tau^{\pm})\in C
and
\mathscr{M}
is indeed compact. It remains to prove (
37
). It is enough to prove

\mathscr{A}_{r}(u_{k}(s),\tau_{k}(s),s)\in\mathinner{[-\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}+\Omega(v^{-}),\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}+\Omega(v^{-})]}\hskip 22.99988pt\forall s\in\mathbb{R}

for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
. As in the proof of
[
AF10
, Lemma 2.8]
we estimate

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\int^{+\infty}_{s_{0}}d\mathscr{A}_{r}(\partial_{s}(u_{k},\tau_{k}),s)ds

\displaystyle=\int^{+\infty}_{s_{0}}\frac{d}{ds}\mathscr{A}_{r}(u_{k},\tau_{k},s)ds-\int^{+\infty}_{s_{0}}(\partial_{s}\mathscr{A}_{r})(u_{k},\tau_{k},s)ds

\displaystyle=\lim_{s\to+\infty}\mathscr{A}_{r}(u_{k},\tau_{k},s)-\mathscr{A}_{r}(u_{k}(s_{0}),\tau_{k}(s_{0}),s_{0})-\int^{+\infty}_{s_{0}}(\partial_{s}\mathscr{A}_{r})(u_{k},\tau_{k},s)ds

\displaystyle=\mathscr{A}_{0}(v^{+},\tau^{+})-\mathscr{A}_{r}(u_{k}(s_{0}),\tau_{k}(s_{0}),s_{0})+\int^{+\infty}_{s_{0}}\dot{\beta}_{r}(s)\int_{0}^{1}F_{t}(u_{k}(s,t))dtds

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{A}_{0}(v^{+},\tau^{+})-\mathscr{A}_{r}(u_{k}(s_{0}),\tau_{k}(s_{0}),s_{0})+\int^{+\infty}_{-\infty}\mathinner{\lVert\dot{\beta}_{r}(s)F\rVert}_{+}ds

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{A}_{0}(v^{+},\tau^{+})-\mathscr{A}_{r}(u_{k}(s_{0}),\tau_{k}(s_{0}),s_{0})+\mathinner{\lVert F\rVert}
